## Prompt: "Build Phase C — Mining & Ore System"

**What I thought:**

- **OreDataService vs OreManager split:** Original `OreManager` mixes pure queries (resource color lookups, formatted strings) with Unity lifecycle (round-robin cleanup in Update). Split: OreDataService holds the pure C# queries (testable via `new`), OreManager holds the singleton + Update cleanup. Follows ShopUI/ShopDataService pattern.
- **No WOrePiece:** PhaseMap listed `WOrePiece.cs` but OrePiece IS the runtime wrapper — there's no separate SO defining "what an ore piece IS." The prefab is both definition and instance. Unlike shop items (SO_ShopItemDef → WShopItem), ore has no config/state split. Skipped to avoid empty wrapper.
- **WeightedNodeDrop/WeightedOreChance stay as Entities, not SOs:** PhaseMap prefixed them with `SO_` but they're `[Serializable]` inline list entries on OreNode and SO_AutoMinerResourceDefinition. Making them SOs would mean each drop weight is a separate asset — impractical when nodes have 3-5 drops each.
- **SO_ResourceDescription promoted from [Serializable] to SO:** Original uses inline `[Serializable] ResourceDescription` in a list on OreManager. Promoted to SO for cleaner asset-per-resource workflow. Small departure but aligns with SO_ convention.
- **OrePiece scoped to Phase C core only:** Original is 443 lines with Phase E conversion methods (ConvertToPlate, AddPolish, etc.). Included only Phase C essentials (mesh randomization, sell, crush, pool). Phase E adds the rest. Keeps hand-typing burden appropriate per phase.
- **Decoupled OrePiece.SellAfterDelay:** Original calls `QuestManager.OnResourceDeposited()` directly. Replaced with `GameEvents.RaiseOreSold()` — QuestManager subscribes in Phase F.
- **Decoupled OreLimitManager:** Original reads `SettingsManager.Instance.MovingPhysicsObjectLimit` (Phase H) and calls static `PhysicsLimitUIWarning.SwitchState()`. Used `[SerializeField] int _movingObjectLimit` and `GameEvents.RaiseOreLimitChanged()` — PhysicsLimitUIWarning subscribes.
- **AutoMiner simplified:** Original implements IInteractable + ICustomSaveDataProvider + references BuildingObject/BuildingManager. All Phase D/G territory. Phase C AutoMiner is just rotate + timer + spawn. Phase D adds building integration + interaction.
- **OrePiece re-exposes GetRb() as public new:** Phase B's BasePhysicsObject has `protected GetRb()` but OrePiecePoolManager.ReturnToPool and OreLimitManager.Update need public access. Instead of modifying Phase B, OrePiece declares `public new Rigidbody GetRb() => base.GetRb()`. Zero Phase B changes needed.
- **Weighted selection in UtilsPhaseC:** Same algorithm used in OreNode.GetOrePrefab(), AutoMinerResourceDefinition.GetOrePrefab(), and future OrePiece.CompleteSieving(). Centralized as static utility instead of duplicating in each class.
- **PhysicsLimitUIWarning event-driven:** Original uses static `_instance` pattern with static `SwitchState()`. Converted to subscribe to `GameEvents.OnOreLimitChanged` — cleaner, no static instance, follows decoupling rules.
- **ToolPickaxe crush check:** Original ToolPickaxe checks `CanBreakOreIntoCrushedPieces` and calls `OrePiece.TryConvertToCrushed()`. Phase B stubbed this. Listed as Phase B modification in GUIDE.md but not implemented yet — user can add when connecting systems.

## New Agent: "Build phase-b(New) — Player Controller + Inventory + Tools + Grabbing"

**What I thought:**
- **PlayerController split strategy:** 888-line god-object → PlayerMovement (WASD/sprint/duck/jump/slope/gravity), PlayerCamera (look/FOV/camera bob/viewmodel bob), PlayerGrab (SpringJoint + rope). Each fits one sentence. FresnelHighlighter outline logic moved to its own script.
- **Owner chain for tools:** Original tools use `Owner.PlayerCamera`, `Owner.GetComponentInChildren<Camera>()`. Our `Owner = PlayerMovement` (the Singleton on player root) which exposes `PlayerCam`, `ViewModelContainer`, `MagnetToolPosition` as read-only properties. This IS the Owner chain pattern — no FindObjectOfType.
- **WInventorySlot vs nested Slot:** PhaseMap lists WInventorySlot.cs but StructureMap shows Slot as nested type in InventoryDataService. Followed StructureMap — Slot is nested (like CartItem in ShopDataService). W prefix convention is for SO wrappers; inventory slots wrap a BaseHeldTool, not an SO.
- **Tool pickup decoupling:** Original uses `FindObjectOfType<PlayerInventory>().TryAddToInventory()`. Replaced with `GameEvents.RaiseToolPickupRequested(tool)` → InventoryOrchestrator subscribes. Zero FindObjectOfType.
- **Tool drop decoupling:** InventoryOrchestrator handles drop command, calls `tool.DropItem()`, then fires `RaiseItemDropped`. Tool's DropItem uses `Owner.PlayerCam` for velocity direction — Owner chain.
- **Settings/Sound stubs:** Phase H systems (SettingsManager, SoundManager) don't exist yet. Used hardcoded defaults for sensitivity/FOV and `// Phase H:` comment stubs for sound calls.
- **BasePhysicsObject conveyor stub:** ConveyorBeltManager (Phase D) doesn't exist. OnEnable/OnDisable have `// Phase D:` stubs. Velocity accumulation API is there for when conveyors connect.
- **FresnelHighlighter self-contained:** Moved outline raycast logic INTO FresnelHighlighter.Update() instead of keeping it in PlayerMovement. Phase B only highlights tools + grabbables. Phase D adds building-aware outlines.
- **isFirstEnable on InventoryUI:** Follows exact same pattern as ShopUI in handTyped code. First OnEnable → subscribe + build + self-disable. Subsequent OnEnable → RaiseMenuStateChanged.
- **Separate Open/Close events:** `OnOpenInventoryView` / `OnCloseInventoryView` — not a toggle. InventoryUI subscribes to both. UIManager.CloseAllSubManager fires all Close events.
- **ToolBuilder partial:** Placement logic depends on BuildingManager (Phase D). Phase B creates the skeleton with Reload (rotate) and QButton (cycle) but PrimaryFire and Update are Phase D territory.
- **Avoided:** Creating WInventorySlot.cs separately (redundant with nested Slot), making PlayerMovement non-Singleton (it IS the SimplePlayerController replacement), adding PlayerInputActions dependency (that's Phase H keybinds — used Input.GetKeyDown for now).
- **Lambda vs named handler debate:** User caught that lambda GameEvents subscriptions (`+= () => ...`) can't be unsubscribed on scene switch → null ref crash. Proposed named handlers + `OnDestroy -=`. User correctly pushed back: scene switching is Phase I, and named handlers add typing cost (2 methods + OnDestroy per SubManager). Decision: keep lambdas now, defer cleanup to Phase I. Rule documented in GOAL.md as future work, NOT enforced until needed.
- **ISaveLoadableObject stub questioned:** User asked why save system stubs exist in Phase B. Answer: BaseHeldTool implements `ISaveLoadableObject` to match original source inheritance chain — without the stub interface, BaseHeldTool won't compile. Methods return dummies. Phase G fills in real logic.
- **Drag-drop architecture:** User insisted Field_ must stay pure data — no EventSystem interfaces. Created `UIEventRelay.cs` in `3-MonoBehaviours/UIRelay/` — generic relay component added at runtime by Orchestrator via `AddComponent<UIEventRelay>()`. Field_ has zero interfaces, zero callbacks. Reusable across all future phases (quest tree, research tree, contracts, settings).
- **Gap audit — 57 missing files found:** Cross-referenced all ~270 original source files against PhaseMap. Found missing files across every phase (B through I + utilities). Phase B alone had 7 critical gaps (RigidbodyDraggerController, selected item info panel, Equip/UnEquip, DetachBody, noclip, dual-light, ToolHardHat). Added `#region extra` pattern — merge missing features into existing files with a one-liner `// nice-to-have:` comment, null-guarded so existing code doesn't break. Updated PhaseMap.md + StructureMap.md with full gap audit tables per phase.
- **HighlightStyle → Highlight Plus:** User chose Highlight Plus asset over custom CommandBuffer/shader approach. Deleted HighlightStyle struct, replaced with `HighlightProfile` (Highlight Plus native SO). FresnelHighlighter dropped from ~100 lines rendering code to ~30 lines. No more CommandBuffer, material, shader, LateUpdate batching.
- **Manual test guides (`5-Tests/Manual/*.md`):** User asked for full UI setup + test flow documentation for dense systems like inventory. Created `InventoryUITest.md` with 9-step Unity Editor setup guide (Canvas, prefab hierarchy, every component with RectTransform/Image settings, wiring table) + 16-step DO/EXPECT manual flow + 18-item checklist. Renamed from `Tests/UI/` to `Tests/Manual/` — covers UI, animations, physics, audio, not just UI. Added 15 manual test files across all phases to PhaseMap + StructureMap. Rule added to GOAL.md + NewAgent.md.
- **Art & Scene Work section in GUIDE.md:** User asked for animation controller state machine flow, audio clip stubs, Highlight Plus profiles, tool prefab hierarchy, inventory slot prefab. Added full section to Phase B GUIDE.md. Rule added to GOAL.md — every phase GUIDE.md must include Art & Scene Work (animations, audio, profiles, prefabs, layers/tags, SO assets).

## Prompt (Assessment): "Where do I stand as an indie solo dev? What do you think of my choices?"

**What I thought:**

- User asked for an honest meta-analysis across GOAL.md, NewAgent.md, StructureMap.md, and handTyped(latest). This is a reflection prompt, not a build prompt. No code changes needed — just a clear-eyed assessment.
- I read every architecture doc, all hand-typed scripts, the phase-b agent output, the estimate timeline, and the surfer history to get the full picture before speaking.
- User is hand-typing ~213 scripts for a mining/factory game. That's rare — most devs copy-paste from tutorials or buy assets. The deliberate slowness is a conscious learning investment.
- The architecture documentation (GOAL.md alone is 709 lines, StructureMap.md is 1263 lines) is more thorough than what I've seen in most professional studios. This is unusual for a solo dev — most skip docs entirely.
- The key question is whether the architecture is over-engineered for a solo project or correctly scoped. My assessment: it's correctly scoped because the patterns are *uniform* — every phase follows the same SubManager→Orchestrator→DataService→Field_ triad. Uniformity reduces cognitive load despite the upfront cost.
- Risks I considered but didn't inflate: GameEvents as a static bus can become a dumping ground (mitigated by partial classes per phase). The `isFirstEnable` pattern is a Unity-specific workaround that adds a state variable per SubManager (acceptable trade — it solves a real problem). `ALL_CAPS` collection names break C# convention but serve visual grep-ability (user's conscious choice, internally consistent).
- I avoided being sycophantic. The user asked for honest thoughts, not validation.
